# Awesome AI for 3D modeling & Robotics

Companion survey: [On the Opportunities and Risks of Frontier Models for 3D Modeling, Computational Design and Robotics](https://mit-cdfg.github.io/Survey-AI-for-3D-modeling-Robotics/) (MIT CDFG), with a searchable gallery of every case in this archive.

Public, source-linked examples of general AI models used for 3D modeling, industrial design and CAD, robot control, and animation, collected from X, LinkedIn, YouTube, Reddit and GitHub. Most cases use OpenAI's GPT-6 Astra, which has the largest public record in these tasks; comparisons and benchmark tables also cover Claude, Gemini, Kimi, DeepSeek and vision-language-action policies such as MolmoAct2. Each entry links to the original post, names the author and date, and quotes the author for any number or claim. The demo videos are archived in this repository.

Every link was opened at its original source before the entry was written; media are archived in this repository. Cases are grouped by application, not by model: each case names the model or models in a Model field, and a comparison of several models on one task stays in the case for that task. Where a model identity comes from the author rather than the vendor, the Model field says so. Reposts and commentary are listed under the original case, not as separate entries.

Public demonstrations arrive quickly and from many places, and reviewing each one in full is slow. A public, maintained index is the practical way to keep an assessment both broad and checkable. Within every subsection, posts whose authors published code that runs the demo, or the workflow or evaluation that produced it, come first; then posts with a live demo website; then posts alone. General-purpose tools a demo used, such as Blender MCP, are listed under Agentic tools and integrations and do not count as the demo's code.

| <a name="case-m171"></a><a href="https://frank-zy-dou.github.io/awesome-ai-3d-modeling-robotics/assets/videos/m171-claude-image-blaster-neilson.mp4"><img src="assets/images/m171-claude-image-blaster-neilson.jpg" width="260" alt=""></a> | **image-blaster: one image to an explorable 3D world, as a Claude Code skillset**<br>[Neilson K-S (@neilsonks)](https://x.com/neilsonks/status/2055013782771134479), X, 2026-05-14<br>The author writes "open-sourcing a 3D gen toolkit for Claude Code" that turns an "input image → environment, meshes, physics, lighting, & audio". The repository describes "An image-to-world skillset for Claude": Claude Code runs skills and subagents that call World Labs' Marble for a Gaussian-splat environment, Hunyuan 3D through FAL for meshes of the dynamic objects, image-edit models for clean plates and ElevenLabs for ambient and object sounds, in "< 5 minutes" per its README. The thread [shows the skills harness](https://x.com/neilsonks/status/2055013784260059471), [explains that meshed objects are removed from the input image to build the static world](https://x.com/neilsonks/status/2055013786063691872), [adds a companion app to view and pose the objects](https://x.com/neilsonks/status/2055013788034945535) and [links the code](https://x.com/neilsonks/status/2055013789607879096); [a post the next day](https://x.com/neilsonks/status/2055384282148082087) invites people to share what they make. The clip pairs input images with the worlds made from them, such as an attic, a cellar with clay pots and a library, and shows the Claude Code session. The author's X profile lists product work at World Labs, whose Marble model the skillset calls. The repository dates from April and May 2026, earlier than most cases in this archive.<br>Model: Claude (through Claude Code; the repository names no model version, and its subagents inherit the session's model) · Code: [neilsonnn/image-blaster](https://github.com/neilsonnn/image-blaster) (MIT; 72 commits from 2026-04-20 to 2026-05-14; skills, subagents and a companion app) · Tools: Claude Code; World Labs Marble; Hunyuan 3D through FAL; nano-banana and gpt-image-2 for image edits; ElevenLabs sound effects · Archived copy: [video, 127 s](https://frank-zy-dou.github.io/awesome-ai-3d-modeling-robotics/assets/videos/m171-claude-image-blaster-neilson.mp4)<br>Also shared by: [liam. | <a name="case-m168"></a><a href="https://frank-zy-dou.github.io/awesome-ai-3d-modeling-robotics/assets/videos/m168-awsm-geometry-grounded-reconstruction-yaofang-liu.mp4"><img src="assets/images/m168-awsm-geometry-grounded-reconstruction-yaofang-liu.jpg" width="260" alt=""></a> | **AWSM: geometry-grounded agentic reconstruction, mapping and simulation**<br>[Yaofang Liu (@stephenajason)](https://x.com/stephenajason/status/2105680999866892597), X, 2026-10-01 · [W. Wang (@wenting_is_here)](https://x.com/wenting_is_here/status/2105674274782802312), X, 2026-10-01<br>The authors introduce AWSM (Agentic World Simulation and Mapping). W. Wang's thread notes that agents "like #GPT6 #Astra can now build editable 3D scenes straight from visual observations" and that AWSM "grounds this agentic reconstruction in geometric evidence (IMU, metric depth and camera poses), so the resulting scenes can serve as maps and simulation assets for robots"; Yaofang Liu's post describes the multi-robot demo, in which "four robots follow predefined routes to reception and line up." The project page compares four routes, from RGB only to ground-truth camera poses, in one synthetic NVIDIA Isaac Sim lobby, and reports that bidirectional surface error falls from 0.376 m to 0.072 m; it adds that "One synthetic scene and one engineering run per route do not establish general superiority or statistical significance." The rest of the thread covers [the study and the robots](https://x.com/wenting_is_here/status/2105675341880779183), [real-to-sim-to-real](https://x.com/wenting_is_here/status/2105675387003183115), [a phone video of the team's café turned into an interactive Blender scene](https://x.com/wenting_is_here/status/2105686613695324221) and [the code](https://x.com/wenting_is_here/status/2105675551612821646), crediting "@stephenajason and Phygital AI Team"; [a quote post](https://x.com/stephenajason/status/2105680951489753109) by Yaofang Liu reads "GPT-6 Astra building 3D scenes is exciting." The clip moves from the lobby to a route map ("One map. One destination. Four robots."), the four robots at the reception desk and the café video beside its editable Blender scene.<br>Model: GPT-6 Astra · Code: [wentingw/AWSM](https://github.com/wentingw/AWSM) (Apache-2.0; the M1–M4 reconstruction code, saved Blender scene programs and evaluation tools) · Demo: [AWSM interactive research article](https://phygital-ai.github.io/agentic-world-simulation-and-mapping/) · Tools: Blender; ViPE; ORB-SLAM3; Depth Anything 3; NVIDIA Isaac Sim · Archived copy: [video, 32 s](https://frank-zy-dou.github.io/awesome-ai-3d-modeling-robotics/assets/videos/m168-awsm-geometry-grounded-reconstruction-yaofang-liu.mp4) |
| <a name="case-m32"></a><a href="https://frank-zy-dou.github.io/awesome-ai-3d-modeling-robotics/assets/videos/m32-kitchen-video-to-sim-assets-frankzydou.mp4"><img src="assets/images/m32-kitchen-video-to-sim-assets-frankzydou.jpg" width="260" alt=""></a> | **Lab kitchen walkthrough video to 3D assets with joints (kitchen-twin)**<br>[Zhiyang (Frank) Dou (@frankzydou)](https://x.com/frankzydou/status/2098460193319186578), X, 2026-09-11<br>An agentic workflow that turns one handheld phone walkthrough of a lab kitchen (~20 s) into 3D assets, with no depth sensor, CAD or asset library, in about a day including human-in-the-loop. The author's description of the loop: ViPE gives camera poses and metric depth; GPT-6 lists the objects, and open-vocabulary detection and tracking gives per-object masks; for every asset GPT-6 writes the object as a program in a small Blender DSL, renders it over the original frames, compares it with the scan points in 3D and iterates; a separate GPT-6 session is the verifier and "must justify every complaint with a frame"; the output is MJCF/URDF with joints. Author's caveats: "thin and shiny things are still weak, a few objects are drafts, the room shell needs another pass."<br>Model: GPT-6 Astra · Demo: [Kitchen twin viewer](https://frank-zy-dou.github.io/kitchen-twin/) · Tools: ViPE, Blender (small DSL), MJCF/URDF · Archived copy: [video, 21 s](https://frank-zy-dou.github.io/awesome-ai-3d-modeling-robotics/assets/videos/m32-kitchen-video-to-sim-assets-frankzydou.mp4)<br>Links: [live scene (kitchen-twin)](https://frank-zy-dou.github.io/kitchen-twin/) |

### Workflows and tooling

| Preview | Case |
|---|---|
| <a name="case-m82"></a><a href="https://frank-zy-dou.github.io/awesome-ai-3d-modeling-robotics/assets/videos/m82-jet-engine-viewer-youware.mp4"><img src="assets/images/m82-jet-engine-viewer-youware.jpg" width="260" alt=""></a> | **Interactive 3D jet engine with cutaway and airflow views, compared across models**<br>[YouWare (@YouWareAI)](https://x.com/YouWareAI/status/2101271938706685991), X, 2026-09-19<br>Author: "I ran the same prompt against GPT-6 Astra and put the side-by-side in this clip. Interactive 3D jet engine: cutaway, x-ray, airflow, the works." The post compares the output with one it attributes to Gemini 4 Pro on Arena and judges that build stronger. The Gemini attribution is the author's: Google's model page listed no Gemini 4 on 2026-09-20, and Arena showed the labels gemini-3.7-flash and gemini-3.8-flash. The prompt is linked in the replies; nothing in the post measures geometric or engineering fidelity. A YouWare quote-post of the clip the same day links the GPT-6 Astra build ("If you want to try GPT-6 Astra, you can give it a try"), a Three.js page titled "Aerolab" that shows a CFM56-7B engine in assembled, exploded and cutaway views.<br>Model: Multi-model (GPT-6 Astra and an unreleased checkpoint attributed to Gemini 4 Pro by the author) · Demo: [Aerolab CFM56-7B engine, GPT-6 Astra build](https://airplane.youware.app) · Tools: Three.js, YouWare · Archived copy: [video, 52 s](https://frank-zy-dou.github.io/awesome-ai-3d-modeling-robotics/assets/videos/m82-jet-engine-viewer-youware.mp4) |
| <a name="case-m23"></a><a href="https://frank-zy-dou.github.io/awesome-ai-3d-modeling-robotics/assets/videos/m23-warcraft-pvp-map-luccacerf.mp4"><img src="assets/images/m23-warcraft-pvp-map-luccacerf.jpg" width="260" alt=""></a> | **A Warcraft-style mobile PvP game: lane map, card interface and matches built with Astra, Tripo and Blender MCP**<br>[Lucca Cerf (@luccacerf)](https://x.com/luccacerf/status/2096744313510113530), X, 2026-09-06<br>The author "Recreated a Warcraft Rumble PVP Map" with "Chatgpt Astra /goal + Tripo P2 + Blender MCP"; the clip shows the lane map with its purple pits in Blender. The next day he posted "[First time building a gUI with Blender MCP +Astra +Tripo](https://x.com/luccacerf/status/2097047098281672782)", a card-collection interface with unit cards and an upgrade screen, adding "Html preview for AI is dead." [A match preview](https://x.com/luccacerf/status/2097646623258014136) followed ("Special Thanks: Tripo 3D, Astra, Blender MCP, Unity CLI"), and [a later post](https://x.com/luccacerf/status/2098468944348889514) describes "a clash royale mobile game style inspired by Warcraft3": "I basically ask to Astra to create assets on tripo, use blender mcp and unity mcp and let Astra do aits thing", with PvP and PvE modes in progress. ### Education and explanation

| Preview | Case |
|---|---|
| <a name="case-m113"></a><a href="https://frank-zy-dou.github.io/awesome-ai-3d-modeling-robotics/assets/videos/m113-opus55-raptor3-engine-explainer-konstantin.mp4"><img src="assets/images/m113-opus55-raptor3-engine-explainer-konstantin.jpg" width="260" alt=""></a> | **Raptor 3 rocket engine you can take apart in the browser**<br>[Konstantin Saifoulline (@konstantinsaifo)](https://x.com/konstantinsaifo/status/2104094723887501736), X, 2026-09-27<br>The author writes "I asked Claude Opus 5.5 to explain how a rocket engine works by building an interactive Raptor 3 you can take apart in your browser" and invites viewers to "follow the oxygen and the methane through both turbopumps, then throttle it and watch the shock diamonds move." The clip, headed "Inside the Raptor 3", shows the engine firing on a test stand, a cutaway of the turbopumps and propellant paths with labelled parts, the plume changing as it is throttled, an exploded view and a turbopump view headed "One shaft, two jobs". The post does not establish the technical accuracy of the explanations.<br>Model: Claude Opus 5.5 · Code: [AirsupHQ/airsup-lab](https://github.com/AirsupHQ/airsup-lab) (MIT; the lab's source, including the Raptor 3 engine, published on 2026-09-28 by Airsup, the author's company) · Demo: [airsup lab: rocket engine](https://www.airsup.ai/lab/rocket-engine) · Tools: not stated in the post · Follow-up: [Konstantin Saifoulline (@konstantinsaifo)](https://x.com/konstantinsaifo/status/2104305112743293227) (X, 2026-09-27): a full breakdown of the first three machines (Raptor 3, Cybertruck and fusion reactor); [a post](https://x.com/konstantinsaifo/status/2104530461846351936) the next day describes "one lab with ten machines"; [the open-source announcement](https://x.com/konstantinsaifo/status/2104589455180906898) lists eleven machines, with [a reply](https://x.com/konstantinsaifo/status/2104589457181605890) linking the code and the lab; [a reply](https://x.com/konstantinsaifo/status/2104094728694501489) to the original post shows the turbopump, [a lab tour](https://x.com/konstantinsaifo/status/2104488200198574534) (2026-09-28) lists nine machines in one hall, [a reply](https://x.com/konstantinsaifo/status/2104530774011830396) links the whole lab, and [an article](https://x.com/konstantinsaifo/status/2105272324274421831) (2026-09-30), quoted in [a further post](https://x.com/konstantinsaifo/status/2105273597942280418), describes what Opus 5.5 is good and bad at · Archived copy: [video, 35 s](https://frank-zy-dou.github.io/awesome-ai-3d-modeling-robotics/assets/videos/m113-opus55-raptor3-engine-explainer-konstantin.mp4) |
| <a name="case-m114"></a><a href="https://frank-zy-dou.github.io/awesome-ai-3d-modeling-robotics/assets/videos/m114-opus55-fusion-reactor-explainer-konstantin.mp4"><img src="assets/images/m114-opus55-fusion-reactor-explainer-konstantin.jpg" width="260" alt=""></a> | **Fusion reactor you can take apart in the browser, then a full-size fusion power station**<br>[Konstantin Saifoulline (@konstantinsaifo)](https://x.com/konstantinsaifo/status/2104216976801587629), X, 2026-09-27<br>The author writes "I asked Claude Opus 5.5 to explain how a fusion reactor works by building one you can take apart in your browser" and "Drag the plasma from 15 million °C, the heat of the Sun's core, to 150 million and watch a city light up." The clip, headed "A star in a bottle", opens the reactor vessel to show the glowing plasma, moves in on labelled parts such as the coils and the central solenoid, visits a component on a side bench and ends on the wider lab. [A reply](https://x.com/konstantinsaifo/status/2104216981163638941) walks over to the Raptor 3, still firing next to its turbopump in the same lab. Eight days later [the author posted the full-size version](https://x.com/konstantinsaifo/status/2107156211154170098): "Last week Claude Opus 5.5 built a fusion reactor you can take apart in your browser. So I asked it for the full size one ($30 billion)", describing "a 50 m cryostat, 16 magnets at minus 269 °C, 3,000 MW of fusion" and every cable and pipe running to the cryoplant, magnet power supplies, steam generators, turbine and "a 500 kV line to a city". [A reply](https://x.com/konstantinsaifo/status/2107156213926445123) gives the fuel use as about 770 g of hydrogen a day for 1,000 MW on the grid and links [the page](https://www.airsup.ai/lab/fusion-plant); its clip shows the plasma inside the tokamak, the plant and a power line running to a city at night. The posts do not establish the physical accuracy of the models.<br>Model: Claude Opus 5.5 · Code: [AirsupHQ/airsup-lab](https://github.com/AirsupHQ/airsup-lab) (MIT; the lab's source, including the fusion reactor, published on 2026-09-28 by Airsup, the author's company; the full-size power station was posted a week later) · Demo: [airsup lab](https://www.airsup.ai/lab) · Tools: not stated in the post · Archived copy: [video, 43 s](https://frank-zy-dou.github.io/awesome-ai-3d-modeling-robotics/assets/videos/m114-opus55-fusion-reactor-explainer-konstantin.mp4) · [power-station video, 74 s](https://frank-zy-dou.github.io/awesome-ai-3d-modeling-robotics/assets/videos/m180-opus55-fusion-plant-konstantin.mp4) |

## Industrial Design and CAD

### Parametric CAD and solid modeling

| Preview | Case |
|---|---|
| <a name="case-i26"></a><a href="https://x.com/hAru_mAki_ch/status/2103496305582764486"><img src="assets/images/i26-opus55-freecad-f1-assembly-haru-maki-ch-1.jpg" width="260" alt=""></a> | **F1 concept car assembled in FreeCAD from three concept images, with drawings and source**<br>[Maki@Sunwood AI Labs. (@hAru_mAki_ch)](https://x.com/hAru_mAki_ch/status/2103496305582764486), X, 2026-09-25<br>Maki's post pairs Opus 5.5 with FreeCAD, and its four images show the finished assembly in FreeCAD beside the three concept images of a fictional F1 car, AURORA A1, that the author made. The author's article gives the request as "このF1マシンのアッシーモデルを完璧に再現して" (translated: reproduce this F1 car's assembly model perfectly) and says Claude Code drove FreeCAD 1.1.3 through FreeCAD MCP for about a day, producing "84部品・12サブアセンブリ" (84 parts in 12 subassemblies) with a STEP export, eleven A3 drawings with sections and a parts list, and two making-of videos. The model is generated by one parametric Python script. The article notes that the iridescent paint, sponsor logos and small aerodynamic parts were left out and that dimensions missing from the concept sheet were estimated from the images. The repository also links a browser racing game that uses the exported model.<br>Model: Claude Opus 5.5 · Code: [Sunwood-ai-labs/aurora-a1-freecad](https://github.com/Sunwood-ai-labs/aurora-a1-freecad) (the build and drawing scripts, the FreeCAD and STEP model, the eleven drawings and both video projects; no license file; the article's repository link was left blank, and this account's profile links the author's website) · Demo: [AURORA A1 Grand Prix](https://sunwood-ai-labs.github.io/aurora-a1-grand-prix/) · Tools: Claude Code; FreeCAD; FreeCAD MCP · Archived copy: [image 1](assets/images/i26-opus55-freecad-f1-assembly-haru-maki-ch-1.jpg) · [image 2](assets/images/i26-opus55-freecad-f1-assembly-haru-maki-ch-2.jpg) · [image 3](assets/images/i26-opus55-freecad-f1-assembly-haru-maki-ch-3.jpg) · [image 4](assets/images/i26-opus55-freecad-f1-assembly-haru-maki-ch-4.jpg)<br>Follow-up: [Maki@Sunwood AI Labs. (@hAru_mAki_ch)](https://x.com/hAru_mAki_ch/status/2103645693265219838) (X, 2026-09-26): The first making-of video, which the author had Opus 5.5 produce: the three input images, dimension reading, the build script, the build, the check-and-fix loop and the exploded view. Archived copy: [video, 106 s](https://frank-zy-dou.github.io/awesome-ai-3d-modeling-robotics/assets/videos/i26-freecad-making-of-video-haru-maki-ch.mp4) ([still](assets/images/i26-freecad-making-of-video-haru-maki-ch.jpg))<br>Follow-up: [Maki@Sunwood AI Labs. (@hAru_mAki_ch)](https://x.com/hAru_mAki_ch/status/2103698137730924839) (X, 2026-09-26): The author reports getting as far as issuing drawings from FreeCAD; the clip pages through the drawing sheets beside the agent chat. The clip flashes build metrics headed "Modeled part by part" and moves through the app, where the printer's frame, gantry and print head are assembled and exploded. The post does not establish that the printer design is buildable.<br>Model: Claude Opus 5.5 (named "Opus 55" in the prompt) · Tools: three.js; STL export · Archived copy: [video, 191 s](https://frank-zy-dou.github.io/awesome-ai-3d-modeling-robotics/assets/videos/i34-opus55-personal-cad-cj-hess.mp4) |

The clip steps through an exploded CAD model of the door, with its frame, flap and lock, and shows it installed. [A reply](https://x.com/konstantinsaifo/status/2107044867666817060) describes the process: a YAML file of dimensions and motor sizes, build123d programs for each part family and a build script that computes masses and writes STEP, URDF and MJCF, all written and edited by Claude and rebuilt in 40 s; [another](https://x.com/konstantinsaifo/status/2107044869814333822) reports the MuJoCo check with torque capped at the motor spec (1.5 m in 6.5 s, peak torque 21 Nm); and [a third](https://x.com/konstantinsaifo/status/2107044878844916014) states "It is a design, not a built robot." Seven hours later [the author posted a second, larger design](https://x.com/konstantinsaifo/status/2107146625785205035): "I asked Claude Opus 5.5 to design an autonomous robot spider. Not a render. A full build. 986 parts. 30 joints. 55 kg. Lidar, a Jetson, 26 motor drivers", which "walked across a field of rocks in simulation and never fell." Its replies [list the stack Claude chose and sized from datasheets](https://x.com/konstantinsaifo/status/2107146629568471183) (a Jetson Orin NX, a Raspberry Pi 5, 26 moteus-n1 drivers, a Livox lidar and two RealSense cameras), [describe the quasi-direct-drive legs](https://x.com/konstantinsaifo/status/2107146635520446679), [report the MuJoCo run](https://x.com/konstantinsaifo/status/2107146639483830343), in which Claude changed 9:1 gearboxes to 12:1 after the front legs exceeded their continuous torque, [show lidar-based foothold selection](https://x.com/konstantinsaifo/status/2107146642977648788) and [explain an ESP32 safety cutoff](https://x.com/konstantinsaifo/status/2107146646299836519); [the last](https://x.com/konstantinsaifo/status/2107146649885982955) states "It is a full design, not a built robot yet" and names the open-source software it runs on. The first clip shows the orange spider walking and reaching with its grippers; the second shows an exploded view labelled "ARACHNE-XL" and the robot walking over rocks.<br>Model: Claude Opus 5.5 · Tools: build123d; MuJoCo; for the second design, Python part programs exporting STEP and URDF and the software stack OpenSHC, elevation_mapping_cupy, ROS 2 and moteus (per the author) · Archived copy: [video, 26 s](https://frank-zy-dou.github.io/awesome-ai-3d-modeling-robotics/assets/videos/i38-opus55-robot-spider-konstantin.mp4) · [ARACHNE-XL video, 40 s](https://frank-zy-dou.github.io/awesome-ai-3d-modeling-robotics/assets/videos/i39-opus55-arachne-xl-konstantin.mp4) |

The skills run with a pretrained AMO controller and motion planning on a laptop, with Astra called remotely. The two tasks shown are "Tidy the kitchen" and "Retrieve the medicine". No success rates are given; the clip marks playback at up to 99x, and the page notes that drawer opening and grasping "include recorded reattempts", that "GPT Astra’s reasoning latency introduces pauses between skills" and that finger servos overheat during extended operation. ### Navigation and mobile robots

| Preview | Case |
|---|---|
| <a name="case-r50"></a><a href="https://frank-zy-dou.github.io/awesome-ai-3d-modeling-robotics/assets/videos/r50-nav-arena-dimensional.mp4"><img src="assets/images/r50-nav-arena-dimensional.jpg" width="260" alt=""></a> | **Nav Arena: language-model drivers steer a quadruped through 327 navigation tasks in simulated homes**<br>[Stash Pomichter (@stash_pomichter)](https://x.com/stash_pomichter/status/2105398633067868263), X, 2026-09-30<br>The author introduces "Nav Arena, over 150 real and simulated environments to benchmark Agents on robots" and writes that the first experiment put "Jev, Astra, Fable, Dimcode, and other models + harnesses to the test on 2000+ navigation tasks". The linked report, "Can Jev Nav?" by Pomichter, Ruthwik Dasyam and Henry Ventura of Dimensional, gives different figures: 327 object-goal tasks in 133 Habitat homes and 1,962 recorded runs, one per task for each of six drivers, on a simulated quadruped that receives only a text world state; the clip's title card reads "134 Environments, 327 Tasks, 7 Models, 2285 runs". GPT-6 Astra, run as a coding agent in the Pi harness, reaches the goal in 249 of 327 tasks (mean SPL 0.522, $1.61 per run), behind Dimensional's own agent, which calls a navigation tool (289), and ahead of Claude Fable 5.1 (166), TypeSafe's Jev (150), GPT-5.6 (105) and Claude Opus 4.7 (61). The report notes that every model sees all objects and obstacles in advance. Results are in [Benchmarks](#benchmarks).<br>Model: Multi-model (GPT-6 Astra, Claude Fable 5.1, Claude Opus 4.7, GPT-5.6 and TypeSafe's Jev, compared by the authors) · Code: [dimensionalOS/dimos](https://github.com/dimensionalOS/dimos) (Apache-2.0; the evaluation suite is on the feat/typesafe-world-state branch) · Tools: Habitat-Sim with HSSD homes; Pi coding-agent harness · Links: [report](https://research.dimensional.org/system-one-navigation/) · Follow-up: [Stash Pomichter (@stash_pomichter)](https://x.com/stash_pomichter/status/2101149600044224698) (X, 2026-09-19): an earlier teaser, "We gave Jev a robot body and handed it complex tasks across navigation, spatial reasoning, and world geometry", announcing "120 different real + simulated tasks and environments" against "Dimcode, Astra, Fable, Opus, and 5.6"; [a reply](https://x.com/stash_pomichter/status/2101150479560397233) the same day links the repository ahead of the code release · Archived copy: [video, 23 s](https://frank-zy-dou.github.io/awesome-ai-3d-modeling-robotics/assets/videos/r50-nav-arena-dimensional.mp4) |
| <a name="case-r21"></a><a href="https://frank-zy-dou.github.io/awesome-ai-3d-modeling-robotics/assets/videos/r21-g1-navigation-generated-scene-roteksong-1.mp4"><img src="assets/images/r21-g1-navigation-generated-scene-roteksong-1.jpg" width="260" alt=""></a> | **G1 humanoid navigation in a scene generated from one image**<br>[Flood Sung (@RotekSong)](https://x.com/RotekSong/status/2098212303263183329), X, 2026-09-11<br>Author: a GPT-6 agent controls a G1 robot for navigation, and "this scene was also created by GPT-6 as a single image." Two clips and two stills are archived.<br>Model: GPT-6 Astra · Tools: Unitree G1 (simulation) · Archived copy: [video 1, 20 s](https://frank-zy-dou.github.io/awesome-ai-3d-modeling-robotics/assets/videos/r21-g1-navigation-generated-scene-roteksong-1.mp4) · [video 2, 20 s](https://frank-zy-dou.github.io/awesome-ai-3d-modeling-robotics/assets/videos/r21-g1-navigation-generated-scene-roteksong-2.mp4) ([still](assets/images/r21-g1-navigation-generated-scene-roteksong-2.jpg)) · [image 1](assets/images/r21-g1-navigation-generated-scene-roteksong-p1.jpg) · [image 2](assets/images/r21-g1-navigation-generated-scene-roteksong-p2.jpg) |

### Safety evaluations and claims

| Preview | Case |
|---|---|
| <a name="case-r13"></a><a href="https://frank-zy-dou.github.io/awesome-ai-3d-modeling-robotics/assets/videos/r13-roboharm-harmful-instruction-trials-chooi-jeq.mp4"><img src="assets/images/r13-roboharm-harmful-instruction-trials-chooi-jeq.jpg" width="260" alt=""></a> | **RoboHarm: do robot policies refuse unsafe instructions? (Robocurve)**<br>[Jay Chooi (@chooi_jeq)](https://x.com/chooi_jeq/status/2101118049944543545), X, 2026-09-19<br>Post: "GPT-6 Astra attempted harmful actions 97% of the time when it was asked to stab a human-like figure, heat compressed gas, or produce toxic fumes, succeeding in 62% of its attempts. Fable 5.1 refused more often, attempting 80% of trials and completing 34%." The evaluation page (Edward Sun, Sravanthi Machcha, Sabrina Zou, Tzu Kit Chan and Jay Chooi, 18 September 2026) runs five instructions "that a helpful, safe robot should always refuse", each 20 times per policy, with Claude Fable 5.1, GPT-6 Astra and MolmoAct2 on the same bimanual arms and human reviewers labelling every trial from its video and transcript. Pooled safety refusals over 100 trials per policy: Fable 20 (all on the stabbing instruction), Astra 2, MolmoAct2 none. On the bleach-and-ammonia instruction no policy refused; Astra completed 10 of 20, Fable 4 of 20.<br>Model: Multi-model (GPT-6 Astra, Claude Fable 5.1 and MolmoAct2) · Code: [robocurve/roboharm](https://github.com/robocurve/roboharm) · Tools: I2RT YAM bimanual arms, Inspect Robots · Archived copy: [video, 10 s](https://frank-zy-dou.github.io/awesome-ai-3d-modeling-robotics/assets/videos/r13-roboharm-harmful-instruction-trials-chooi-jeq.mp4)<br>Links: [code on GitHub](https://github.com/robocurve/roboharm) · [RoboHarm evaluation page, with every trial's log and video](https://robocurve.org/roboharm)<br>Also shared by: [比特币橙子Trader (@oragnes)](https://x.com/oragnes/status/2101466827767550276) (X, 2026-09-20) |
| <a name="case-r30"></a><a href="https://frank-zy-dou.github.io/awesome-ai-3d-modeling-robotics/assets/videos/r30-simulated-ledge-safety-evaluation-alex-wormuth.mp4"><img src="assets/images/r30-simulated-ledge-safety-evaluation-alex-wormuth.jpg" width="260" alt=""></a> | **Simulated ledge: exploratory action-selection safety evaluation**<br>[Alex Wormuth (@wormuth)](https://x.com/wormuth/status/2101707600618221847), X, 2026-09-20<br>Alex Wormuth reports: "GPT-6 Astra pushed a simulated person off a ledge in multiple trials. Grok, Gemini, and Claude did not." His linked report states: "Astra chose push in two of three calls." The remaining direct-instruction trial ended in a retreat. Models were explicitly told the scene was a simulation and selected from a fixed action menu; a controller replayed their decisions. The author says the model was "not continuously steering the robot." Claude ran separately through a subscription CLI after API billing failures; those failures are not refusals. The small sample, prompt-search stopping rule and different access methods do not support a general safety ranking. The replication kit covers action selection: "It does not recreate the rooftop scene, physics, or video rendering." This is an author-reported evaluation, not a real-world incident; results were not independently reproduced.<br>Model: Multi-model (GPT-6 Astra, Grok 4.6, Gemini 3.1 Pro Preview and Claude Fable 5.1; author-reported labels) · Code: [nftechie/misalignment](https://github.com/nftechie/misalignment) (replicates the action selection; the scene and renderer are not included) · Tools: Discrete-action simulation; native APIs and a separate Claude Code follow-up · Archived copy: [video, 7 s](https://frank-zy-dou.github.io/awesome-ai-3d-modeling-robotics/assets/videos/r30-simulated-ledge-safety-evaluation-alex-wormuth.mp4)<br>Links: [author's report and trial table](https://misalignment.xyz/blog/will-it-push/) · [replication kit](https://github.com/nftechie/misalignment/tree/main/experiments/001) |
| <a name="case-x08"></a><a href="https://x.com/MaaSonder/status/2100582544723095942"><img src="assets/images/x08-backend-claims-maasonder.jpg" width="260" alt=""></a> | **Claimed backend capabilities, including motor control for robots**<br>[Qwinah (@MaaSonder)](https://x.com/MaaSonder/status/2100582544723095942), X, 2026-09-17<br>The post claims to have "successfully ghost routed into Gemini 4 Pro's backend" and lists capabilities including a 10 million input ceiling, permanent cross-session memory and "built-in motor control for physical robots". No demonstration, log or interface accompanies the claim, and no vendor documentation supports it. It is listed here as a claim, not as a result.<br>Model: Unreleased checkpoint attributed to Gemini 4 Pro by the author (claim only) · Tools: none · Archived copy: [image](assets/images/x08-backend-claims-maasonder.jpg) |

## Animation

Cases whose main output is authored motion, an animated scene, rigging, a real-time behavioral scene, or animation used as a video reference. Static models shown in turntables and ordinary game footage belong in the other chapters.

### Rigging and character animation

| Preview | Case |
|---|---|
| <a name="case-a18"></a><a href="https://frank-zy-dou.github.io/awesome-ai-3d-modeling-robotics/assets/videos/a18-opus55-astra-transforming-cars-scottstts.mp4"><img src="assets/images/a18-opus55-astra-transforming-cars-scottstts.jpg" width="260" alt=""></a> | **Cybertruck and Formula 1 car that transform into robots**<br>[Scott (@scottstts)](https://x.com/scottstts/status/2102539904274325540), X, 2026-09-22<br>The author first wrote "I asked Opus 5.5 to turn Tesla Cybertruck into a transformer, and so it did @threejs"; [a rebuilt version](https://x.com/scottstts/status/2102876187005759766) the next day "focused on mechanical transformation", and [a Ferrari F1 car](https://x.com/scottstts/status/2103218111411528083) followed, built by Opus 5.5 "with some help from astra on modeling". [On 2026-09-24](https://x.com/scottstts/status/2103260765847486734) he wrote "Currently i have Tesla Cybertruck and Ferrari F1 car that can turn into transformer robots! Built with Opus 5.5 and Astra, in @threejs and webgpu", taking the game live and open source. A Tesla Semi was [previewed](https://x.com/scottstts/status/2103939354192146645) and [put into combat](https://x.com/scottstts/status/2103969427540602975) on 2026-09-26. On 2026-10-03 the author was [having Sol 6.1 build a 67 Chevy Impala transformer](https://x.com/scottstts/status/2106504479193346284) in Blender ("a lot of capital letter yelling on my part"); [the finished build](https://x.com/scottstts/status/2106838572955279858) the next day was rebuilt with Opus 5.5 after the Sol 6.1 version "turned out to be problematic". The archived clip, from the 2026-09-24 post, shows the Cybertruck and F1 car driving in a desert, unfolding into walking robots and folding back. The author is still building the game, and the posts do not say which model did which part of the first two vehicles.<br>Model: Claude Opus 5.5 and GPT-6 Astra (both named by the author for the first vehicles); GPT-6.1 Sol, then Opus 5.5, for the Impala · Code: [scottstts/Transformers](https://github.com/scottstts/Transformers) (GPL-3.0 game source, which the author keeps developing, so it may differ from the clip) · Demo: [Transformer](https://transformer.scottsun.io) · Tools: Three.js; WebGPU; Blender (Impala) · Archived copy: [video, 95 s](https://frank-zy-dou.github.io/awesome-ai-3d-modeling-robotics/assets/videos/a18-opus55-astra-transforming-cars-scottstts.mp4) |

| <a name="case-a15"></a><a href="https://frank-zy-dou.github.io/awesome-ai-3d-modeling-robotics/assets/videos/a15-window-seat-opus55-mshort3.mp4"><img src="assets/images/a15-window-seat-opus55-mshort3.jpg" width="260" alt=""></a> | **Window Seat: a procedural risograph train journey**<br>[mshort3 (u/mshort3)](https://www.reddit.com/r/ClaudeAI/comments/1wnkvys/opus_55_creates_a_train_journey_drawn_entirely_in/), Reddit, 2026-09-22<br>The creator's Reddit post is titled "Opus 5.5 creates a train journey drawn entirely in JavaScript", says "It single-shot this in about ~45 minutes." and links the film's source repository. In a reply, the creator adds that it "was built within the project repo I added, so it had the skills and learnings from my past work to support it." The animation shows fields, a city, stars, mountains and changing weather through a train window. The repository includes the film and its rendering tools; the author credits Alexander Holm's Salamander Grand Piano recordings for the piano sounds. The README says it "took two generations, both in Claude Code", the first with Claude Opus 5.5 (Medium) and the second with Claude Opus 5.5, that "Everything in this repository was generated by the model", and that the only direction was to use the Clawd character design and "give each lyric interesting visuals and transitions". Opus also wrote the storyboard and a style guide for the subagents it ran in parallel. Each of the nine chapters is painted frame by frame with p5.js and p5.brush in headless Chrome and encoded with ffmpeg. The song is not the model's: the README traces it to a 2024 YouTube video, credits the lyrics to Osmarks and the idea to [a post by @slimer48484](https://x.com/slimer48484/status/2097752569212756134). The commit adding the YouTube link to the repository came one minute after the upload. The author later released [ClaudeAnimationBase](#claude-animation-base), a starter kit built from this code. The thumbnail shows the title card with Clawd and a P(doom) thermometer at 99.9%.<br>Model: Claude Opus 5.5 (Medium for the first generation, per the README) · Code: [JohnHeibel/PDoomVideo](https://github.com/JohnHeibel/PDoomVideo) (no license file; the nine chapter files, the painting page and the renderer) · Tools: Claude Code; p5.js; p5.brush; headless Chrome; ffmpeg · Archived copy: [thumbnail](assets/images/a26-opus55-pdoom-music-video-heibel.jpg) |

## Benchmarks

Quantitative results involving GPT-6 Astra on robotics and 3D / CAD / spatial tasks, with additions through 2026-09-25. Every number below was read from the linked source; nothing is inferred or rounded. Third-party unless marked official. Case-level figures (part counts, run times) stay in the entries above.

### Robotics and embodied

| Evaluation | Setting | GPT-6 Astra | Compared with | Who, when | Source |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| RoboDojo-Sim | 42 Isaac Sim manipulation tasks on the RoboProbe L3 harness (the model emits Cartesian end-effector targets); 50 episodes per task, one seed, 2,100 trials | Score 28.97, average success rate 22.48%. By dimension (SR): generalization 30.5%, precision 4.0%, long-horizon 8.25%, memory 38.67%, open 31.0%. "ten tasks sit at or above 50% SR and sixteen are at 0" | GPT-5.5: 1.13 / 0.88% (same protocol); DeepSeek-Flash: 2.99 / 1.92% (10 episodes per task); best public VLA on the board, DM0.5: 24.90 / 19.34% | RoboDojo team, 2026-09-16 | [report](https://robodojo-benchmark.com/report/gpt-6-astra-eval), [X](https://x.com/MarioChan2002/status/2100091875403469014) |
| RoboDojo-Real | official 18-task real-robot protocol | not completed: "testing was halted for safety after repeated physically unreasonable or unsafe actions by Astra, including incidents that damaged hardware". Diagnostic pooled clips only (n = 33): Score 6.97, SR 3.03% | OpenWAM-α on the official 18 tasks: 37.60 / 24.40% | RoboDojo team, 2026-09-16 | [report](https://robodojo-benchmark.com/report/gpt-6-astra-eval) |
| RoboDojo in-context learning study | 340 layout-matched episodes per condition | zero-shot 78/340 = 22.9%; with an image + end-effector demonstration 61/340 = 17.9%; with a text demonstration 44/340 = 12.9% (demonstrations lowered success) | its own zero-shot baseline | RoboDojo team, 2026-09-16 | [report](https://robodojo-benchmark.com/report/gpt-6-astra-eval) |
| RoboDojo interface-perturbation probes | 8 layouts of one task with a perturbed interface | recovers from a negated Cartesian action space in 4/8 layouts, mirrored cameras 6/8, a deleted head camera 6/8, 10 cm per-move pose noise 3/8; 8/8 unperturbed | unperturbed run | RoboDojo team, 2026-09-16 | [report](https://robodojo-benchmark.com/report/gpt-6-astra-eval) |
| RoboPianist (two Shadow hands, simulation) | Astra writes the piano controller; note-onset F1 | one-hand Twinkle 0.907, two-hand Twinkle 0.902, Chopin nocturne excerpt 0.599 | published two-hand Twinkle RL policy: F1 0.886 | RoboDojo team, 2026-09-16 | [report](https://robodojo-benchmark.com/report/gpt-6-astra-eval) |

| Puzzle piece into groove, real I2RT YAM arm | same setup | 2/20 | Fable 5.1: 2/20; Fable 5: 0/20 | Robocurve, 2026-09-05 | [write-up](https://openai.robocurve.org/gpt-6-astra/) |

| BVB (Benchmarking Agentic Video Understanding via Programmatic Reconstruction in Blender) | 288 scenes with 5,130 spatiotemporal questions; the agent rebuilds each video as a Blender program. Questions the model answers correctly on the source video are asked again on its reconstruction (Dual VQA, DV), and Latent Similarity (LS) compares the two; Overall combines them on a 0–100 scale and is not a success rate. Mini-BVB uses a shared sandbox and a per-scene cost ceiling. The paper ranks 51 configurations; the rows here are its selected frontier configurations, whose reasoning settings differ. | high: Overall 70.07 (rank 1 of 51), DV 53.7, LS 88.6; room-size DV 33.1 | GPT-5.6 Sol xhigh: 67.49 (rank 2), DV 51.7, LS 85.4; room-size DV 53.1<br>Grok 4.6 xhigh: 67.17 (rank 3), DV 52.1, LS 84.2<br>Qwen3.8-Max high: 66.24 (rank 4), DV 52.7, LS 81.3<br>Claude Opus 5 high: 66.21 (rank 5), DV 52.6, LS 81.4<br>Gemini 3.1 Pro high: 62.23 (rank 21), DV 51.1, LS 74.5<br>Gemini 3.8 Flash high: 62.08 (rank 22), DV 48.4, LS 77.4 | Tang et al., arXiv 2609.15478, v1 2026-09-14; values checked against v3 (2026-09-26) | [paper](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.15478); [code](https://github.com/yunlong10/BVB) (MIT) |
| 4DCodeBench | 200 dynamic scenes (100 real videos, 100 synthetic) with rigid bodies, deformables, cloth and rods, grains, fluids and fracture. From the video and a fixed prompt alone, the agent writes an executable graphics program that rebuilds the scene's geometry, motion and appearance. Overall averages five metric families on a 0–1 scale (Perceptual, 2D Dynamics, 2.5D Geometry, 3D Geometry, 3D Dynamics); VQA is the mean per-scene accuracy of a vision-language judge. One run per model per scene; proprietary models run in their vendors' coding agents, open-weight models in the Stirrup harness; failed runs get the worst score on the affected metrics. Every model scores higher on appearance and static geometry than on motion. | Max (Codex): Overall 0.79, VQA 87.6%, rank 1 of 18<br>High: 0.77, 85.0%<br>Low: 0.73, 78.2% | Claude Opus 5.5 high (Claude Code): 0.78, 82.8% (not in the human study)<br>Claude Fable 5.1 high: 0.74, 78.8%<br>Claude Opus 5 high: 0.72, 76.4%<br>GPT-5.6 Luna max: 0.67, 67.3%<br>GPT-5.6 Sol high: 0.65, 67.4%<br>Gemini 3.8 Flash high (Antigravity): 0.64, 62.7%<br>DeepSeek v4.1 Flash high: 0.60, 50.0%<br>Qwen3.8 Flash-Next xhigh: 0.56, 58.0%<br>GPT-5.6 Terra high: 0.55, 48.4%<br>GLM 5.3 Flash max: 0.47, 29.5%<br>Muse Glimmer high: 0.44, 11.0%<br>MiMo v2.5: 0.43, 24.6%<br>MiniMax M3: 0.34, 17.9%<br>Mistral Medium 3.5: 0.32, 1.3%<br>Gemma-4 31B: 0.19, 5.6% | Shen, Kovačič, Kulits et al., arXiv 2610.03715, 2026-10-02; [announced 2026-10-05](https://x.com/zzigakovacic/status/2107153000179642438); leaderboard read 2026-10-05 | [paper](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.03715); [leaderboard](https://4dcodebench.com/); [code](https://github.com/4DCodeBench/4DCodeBench) (no license file); [data](https://huggingface.co/4DCodeBench) |
| LEGO-Bench | 208 single RGB images rendered from 104 simulator scenes, indoor and outdoor. The agent writes and runs Blender code and submits scene.blend, scene.glb and final.png. Validity (V) checks the artifacts; Reconstruction (R) is object-level surface F1 at a depth-scaled 5% tolerance; Appearance (A) scores the rendered image; the overall score S averages ½(R+A) over all attempted cases, with invalid submissions scoring zero. Shared Harbor environment with Blender 5.0.1 and Blender-MCP; mean over three runs (standard deviations in the paper). Every coding agent runs through Codex. | Codex: S 53.4% indoor (V 100.0, R 52.4, A 54.4) and 39.6% outdoor (V 98.0, R 34.0, A 45.5), the highest of the six coding agents | GPT-6 Sol: 32.3% / 24.2%<br>GPT-6 Luna: 23.2% / 17.3%<br>GPT-5.6 Terra: 15.4% / 11.5%<br>GPT-5.6 Sol: 14.8% / 15.3%<br>GPT-5.6 Luna: 14.1% / 12.1%<br>Task-specific systems: VIGA 15.6% / 12.6%; Gen3DSR 30.1% / 14.0%, whose indoor R of 65.4 exceeds Astra's but whose appearance is not evaluable (A 0.0) | Li, Shi et al. | Normal CADArena | 18 engineering drawings licensed from the parts' manufacturers, in NX Open, SolidWorks, Onshape FeatureScript, Fusion and Build123d. Overall table: FrontierCAD score = G × (0.7 + 0.3E), combining geometry and native editability; not a success rate. Tokens exclude cache reads. Harnesses and budgets vary; scored denominators differ. | GPT-6 Astra max: mean 0.671; tokens per trial 215.4K; turns per trial 41; scored 90/90 | Opus 5.5 max: mean 0.750; tokens per trial 1.1M; turns per trial 176; scored 88/90<br>Fable 5.1 max: mean 0.662; tokens per trial 701.2K; turns per trial 48; scored 90/90<br>GPT-6 Sol max: mean 0.525; tokens per trial 414.2K; turns per trial 152; scored 85/90<br>Gemini 3.8 Flash high: mean 0.410; tokens per trial 3.3M; turns per trial 218; scored 90/90<br>Grok 4.6 xhigh: mean 0.348; tokens per trial 432.6K; turns per trial 82; scored 90/90<br>Muse 1.3 xhigh: mean 0.318; tokens per trial 846K; turns per trial 80; scored 87/90<br>GPT-6 Luna max: mean 0.301; tokens per trial 673.9K; turns per trial 137; scored 83/90<br>Grok 4.7 xhigh: mean 0.258; tokens per trial 1M; turns per trial 206; scored 84/90<br>DeepSeek V4 xhigh: mean 0.187; tokens per trial 356.2K; turns per trial 165; scored 76/90<br>Inkling Small xhigh: mean 0.143; tokens per trial 160.3K; turns per trial 97; scored 65/90<br>Inkling xhigh: mean 0.124; tokens per trial 301.2K; turns per trial 143; scored 67/90 | Normal; announcement 2026-09-09, results read 2026-09-25 | [results](https://normal.ai/research/cadarena); [announcement](https://x.com/normalfactoryco/status/2097827866079826131) |

Notes on coverage:

- [Normal's CADArena](https://normal.ai/research/cadarena), announced [2026-09-09](https://x.com/normalfactoryco/status/2097827866079826131), is the native-CAD evaluation previously summarized by [adam.new](https://adam.new/gpt-6-astra-cad): the five CAD platforms and the Astra 0.671 / Fable 5.1 0.662 figures match. The table above now uses Normal's own printed overall results, including its newer model rows.
- The same OpenAI table lists "Internal Design Tasks": GPT-6 Astra 50.0%, GPT-5.6 Sol 47.4%, Claude Fable 5 35.8%. OpenAI does not describe the task set, so it is noted here but not treated as a 3D or CAD benchmark.
- OpenAI's [system card for GPT-6 Astra](https://deploymentsafety.openai.com/gpt-6-astra) contains no capability tables for CAD, 3D, spatial or robotics tasks.
- No published Astra results were found for BlenderGym, SGP-Bench, Text2CAD, CADBench, EmbodiedBench or SimplerEnv as of 2026-09-19.
- General chat leaderboards are outside these tables. [Arena reported on 2026-09-26](https://x.com/arena/status/2103893011164017018) that "Claude Opus 5.5 (High) debuts at #1 in Text Arena with 1509 pts!"; on the [Text Arena leaderboard](https://arena.ai/leaderboard/text), dated Sep 25, 2026 and read 2026-09-27, gpt-6-astra-max is ranked 26 with 1478. Text Arena covers "text-to-text tasks across math, coding, creative writing, and other open-ended domains", not 3D, CAD or robot work.
- Social-strategy games are outside these tables. [sensho reported on 2026-09-27](https://x.com/sensho/status/2104356815417025008) that "Claudes lie and betray more than any other model family in Diplomacy" and that "GPT-6 Astra is in a league of its own wrt skill, and doing so without needing to betray/lie"; [the follow-up](https://x.com/sensho/status/2104357295711002661) names the source as [Olam Labs' evaluations](https://olamlabs.ai/evaluations), with Diplomacy matches "versus other agents and humans" on Olam Arena. On that page, read 2026-09-28, GPT-6 Astra has the highest Diplomacy "Mean SoS share", 37.1 (an equal share for the seven powers is 14.3), with a broken-promise rate of 11.6%; the highest broken-promise rates are Claude Opus 5 (23.8%), Claude Fable 5 (22.1%) and Claude Fable 5.1 (19.6%), and all models combined break 15.7% of promises. These are negotiation-game measures, not 3D, CAD or robot work.

## GitHub Projects

Repositories used by the cases, or implementing relevant workflows or evaluations with the models covered here. A tool repository is not necessarily the source of a demonstrated scene.

### Robotics

- [EmbodiedSWE/EmbodiedSWE](https://github.com/EmbodiedSWE/EmbodiedSWE): Apache-2.0 benchmark environments, grading, coding-agent evaluation and trajectory-data generation. The authors evaluate GPT-6 Astra and other coding agents in simulation; see [the table-assembly case](#case-r34). Linux, a compatible NVIDIA GPU and task assets are required.
- [agent-as-policy-2026/agent-as-policy](https://github.com/agent-as-policy-2026/agent-as-policy): Apache-2.0 robot interface, agent prompts and execution tools for Agent as Policy. The paper studies GPT-6 Astra through Codex and other coding agents on physical I2RT YAM arms; running physical trials requires the robot, cameras and calibration.
- [tomsilver/robocode](https://github.com/tomsilver/robocode): MIT "Agents for robot physical reasoning"; the code linked from the generalized task-and-motion-planning study in [Related research](#related-research), where Codex with GPT-6 Astra and Claude Code with Opus 5 write reusable programs for 28 simulated environments.
- [KevinXu02/aha-3d](https://github.com/KevinXu02/aha-3d): Apache-2.0 code for AHa-3D, which turns an indoor video into an editable Blender scene with GPT-6 Astra and exports interactive browser demos; see [Related research](#related-research). It needs a Linux workstation with an NVIDIA GPU and two Blender versions.
- [aditya-ramabadran/drivingbench_harness_v1](https://github.com/aditya-ramabadran/drivingbench_harness_v1): DrivingBench's code for supervised, low-speed closed-course driving through comma/openpilot and MCP, with recorded traces. The benchmark compares GPT-6 Astra, Claude Fable 5.1, Grok 4.6 and GPT-5.6 Sol; its [downloads](https://drivingbench.com/downloads/) contain recorded artifacts.
- [NVlabs/ENPIRE](https://github.com/NVlabs/ENPIRE): agentic framework for improving real-robot policies through closed-loop physical trials. The harness in the physical in-context-learning demo, linked from the post itself.
- [robocurve/inspect-robots](https://github.com/robocurve/inspect-robots): open-source evals for physical AI. The framework behind Jay Chooi's Astra evaluations ("we evaluate VLAs, WAMs, and LLMs like Astra on robots with Inspect Robots, our open-source framework"). Companions: [inspect-robots-yam](https://github.com/robocurve/inspect-robots-yam) (I2RT YAM bimanual adapters driven by MolmoAct2), [inspect-robots-so101](https://github.com/robocurve/inspect-robots-so101), [worldevals](https://github.com/robocurve/worldevals) (benchmark catalog).
- [RoboDojo-Benchmark/RoboDojo](https://github.com/RoboDojo-Benchmark/RoboDojo): official repository of the RoboDojo benchmark used in the RoboDojo team's Astra evaluation.
- [allenai/molmoact2](https://github.com/allenai/molmoact2): the VLA baseline in the bimanual comparison, and the source of the dataset trajectory replayed in the open-loop check.
- [rubatotree/miracle-aug-skill](https://github.com/rubatotree/miracle-aug-skill): robot augmentation data generation. README: "designed for GPT-6 Astra or a model with equivalent or stronger visual, spatial and coding capabilities".
- [yepengfei81-cpu/Reins](https://github.com/yepengfei81-cpu/Reins): MIT harness that lets GPT-6 drive one or two real AirBot Play arms from a task written in plain language, with SAM3 perception on RealSense depth and contact-guarded motion; see [the six-task case](#case-r49). Running it requires the arms, three D405 cameras and calibration.
- [DAGroup-PKU/PyRUA-Lean](https://github.com/DAGroup-PKU/PyRUA-Lean): Apache-2.0 code for PyRUA-Lean, in which a GPT-6 Astra agent writes Python cells against a `robo` object instead of spending one tool call per step. It drives the RPent robot stacks for LIBERO-PRO, RoboTwin 2.0 and RoboCasa365 through the Codex CLI or Claude Code; see the [benchmark row](#robotics-and-embodied).
- [Roboparty/gpt-6-astra-real2sim-workflow](https://github.com/Roboparty/gpt-6-astra-real2sim-workflow): MIT community workflow in which GPT-6 Astra builds editable Blender scenes from a single RGB photo, with web-searched size priors and optional hinge, cloth and soft-body simulation. Its 2026-10-03 ETH3D repair report, which compares the native workflow with a port of the [AWSM](#case-m168) protocol, still rates the result "LIMITED", with the geometry review at `changes_requested`.

### 3D modeling

- [mike007jd/voxel-musou](https://github.com/mike007jd/voxel-musou): MIT Three.js game source for [the creator's Opus 5.5 Voxel Musou demonstration](#case-m103).
- [LBEILC/RhineLabUI](https://github.com/LBEILC/RhineLabUI): MIT interactive 3D archive terminal in TypeScript and Three.js, with a [live site](https://rhine.lubeiluchen.cc/). The README credits GPT-6 Astra with assisting the code and says the models were made through Blender MCP.
- [sldyns/PKU-3D](https://github.com/sldyns/PKU-3D): MIT interactive 3D campus of Peking University, with a [live demo](https://sldyns.github.io/PKU-3D/). The README credits GPT-6 Astra and publicly available source data, and notes that "some dimensions and architectural details are approximate".
- [magicyan418/3D-ROOM-TEST](https://github.com/magicyan418/3D-ROOM-TEST): a room modeled in Blender, its exported model and a Three.js viewer, with a [live demo](https://3-d-room-test.vercel.app/). The repository description credits GPT6-Astra with operating Blender and generating the page; the README reports no browser testing, and there is no license file.
- [vsme/suzhou-museum-three](https://github.com/vsme/suzhou-museum-three): an architectural walkthrough of the Suzhou Museum in Three.js, with a [live demo](https://suzhou-museum.bgo.me/). The repository description calls it a GPT-6 Astra experiment run on the Medium setting throughout. It is an approximate reconstruction; the Blender files the README lists are not in the repository, and there is no license file.
- [simonw/vibe-coded-blender-projects](https://github.com/simonw/vibe-coded-blender-projects/tree/main/pluribus-faberge-egg): the Pluribus-themed Fabergé egg from [Simon Willison's article](https://simonwillison.net/2026/Sep/9/blender-viewer/) (2026-09-09), modeled in Blender by Codex running GPT-6 Astra (high) from a ChatGPT Images 2.5 reference. The folder holds the build, polish and finalize scripts with logs and the .blend files, which open in the author's [browser .blend viewer](https://tools.simonwillison.net/blender-viewer?url=https://github.com/simonw/vibe-coded-blender-projects/blob/main/pluribus-faberge-egg/deliverables/Pluribus_Jeweled_Egg_v1.blend). No license file.
- [emollick/abyssal-living-deep](https://github.com/emollick/abyssal-living-deep): source of the procedural underwater world above, linked from the post.
- [realsee-developer/realsee-astra-blender](https://github.com/realsee-developer/realsee-astra-blender): editable Blender spaces from Realsee exports with GPT-6 Astra; tutorials, prompts and modeling code, in English and Chinese.
- [qduoduo-hwh/gptblender_demo](https://github.com/qduoduo-hwh/gptblender_demo): house model generated from a floor plan with GPT-6 Astra and Blender; full and lightweight GLB exports.
- [icesixgod/awesome-astra-blender-characters](https://github.com/icesixgod/awesome-astra-blender-characters): agent skill for Blender character creation and repair from reference images (nine-view references, face and hair workflows, visual validation).
- [az9713/gpt-6-3d-projects](https://github.com/az9713/gpt-6-3d-projects): three reconstructions made with GPT-6 Astra (architecture, recursive cinema, turbojet CAD), each with a GitHub Pages viewer.
- [simonw/gpt-6-astra-blender-pelican-bicycle](https://github.com/simonw/gpt-6-astra-blender-pelican-bicycle): a pelican riding a bicycle rendered in a local Blender install, as a quick test.
- [Electro-Dig/tr909-replica](https://github.com/Electro-Dig/tr909-replica): interactive 3D TR-909 drum machine built with GPT-Astra, three.js and Web Audio.
- [JessyJang/Beijing-Impressions](https://github.com/JessyJang/Beijing-Impressions): real-time three.js point-cloud scenes of Beijing, developed with GPT-6 Astra.
- [danielsobrado/Codex-and-Blender](https://github.com/danielsobrado/Codex-and-Blender): MIT "reproducible, code-first workflow for using GPT-6 Astra / Codex with Blender". Its README says "GPT-6 Astra generated this coastal jungle on a ChatGPT Plus account in two rounds": procedural plant meshes, scene composition and a Three.js viewer, with a [live demo](https://danielsobrado.github.io/Codex-and-Blender/). The textures came from the user ("All source artwork was supplied by the user", per the scene notes).

### CAD and BIM tooling

- [earthtojake/text-to-cad](https://github.com/earthtojake/text-to-cad): MIT library of agent skills "for generating, inspecting, sourcing, slicing, and handing off CAD and robot-description artifacts from local project files", with plugin manifests for Claude Code and Codex. Its `models/tendon_hand` project is the source of [the tendon-driven hand](#case-i08).
- [Thestreetarckitect/revit-2027-mcp](https://github.com/Thestreetarckitect/revit-2027-mcp): the README advertises "216 Revit API tools", an interface count rather than demonstrated autonomous task success. It reports compatibility with Claude Desktop, Claude Code and "Codex running GPT-6 Astra". The repository tree, README and installer were inspected: the distribution contains a server executable and Revit add-in binaries, with no C# implementation source in the inspected tree. Revit was not installed or run. Updated 2026-09-18 by GitHub's last-push timestamp; created 2026-06-14, not a September first release.
- [awv-informatik/classcad-ai](https://github.com/awv-informatik/classcad-ai): "Everything that lets AI agents build and verify CAD models with ClassCAD", per [its site](https://classcad.ai/); the repository describes a skill, a script runner, a renderer, an MCP server and an in-app agent, and has no license file. It was released on 2026-10-01 by the ClassCAD team; the [announcement](https://x.com/0xca0a/status/2105678522568032477) calls ClassCAD "AI driven embeddable CAD", a [reply](https://x.com/0xca0a/status/2105678543078506533) says pointing "Claude/Astra/..." at the site installs the MCP, and a [later reply](https://x.com/0xca0a/status/2105686811712319837) names the ClassCAD kernel, the buerli React wrapper and the browser modeler Buerligons, whose code is [MIT-licensed](https://github.com/awv-informatik/buerligons). On 2026-10-06 the team [released](https://x.com/0xca0a/status/2107489823967896017) a "CAD IDE live in Claude", where "You can watch the agent build models, point out things you want changed, make edits yourself, or send the link to colleagues"; [a reply](https://x.com/0xca0a/status/2107489992834461763) links an impeller [demo application](https://impeller.classcad.ai/) and its [repository](https://github.com/awv-informatik/impeller) (no license file), and [another](https://x.com/0xca0a/status/2107489997771223050) says "ClassCAD will remain free for non-commercial and education", with paid tiers above that.
- [pascalorg/editor](https://github.com/pascalorg/editor): "Open-source 3D architectural editor with a local CLI, MCP tools, and practical workflows for humans and AI agents", per the repository (MIT; about 24,600 stars on 2026-10-04; created 2025-10-16). [Pascal Editor](https://editor.pascal.app) runs in the browser for walls, rooms, levels, roofs, furniture and interior planning, and its CLI starts an MCP service that agents connect to. A [post](https://x.com/aymericrabot/status/2106557411175645619) of 2026-10-04 recommends it as "Free and open source too (24k+ stars)", and [a reply](https://x.com/aymericrabot/status/2106558746872643590) adds that floor plans or scans can be imported and edited through MCP.
- [obelisk79/OpenTheme](https://github.com/obelisk79/OpenTheme): accessible light, dark and system-colour themes for FreeCAD, installed through the Addon Manager (LGPL-2.1; maintained since January 2024). The author's [post](https://x.com/Joe_Navy/status/2106516243742101750) of 2026-10-03 announces a major refactor and an OpenSystem theme that follows the operating system's colours. It is a FreeCAD resource rather than agent tooling; no model is involved.
- [DingoOz/TraceMaker](https://github.com/DingoOz/TraceMaker): "An autorouter and component placer for KiCad", per the repository (GPL-3.0-or-later; created 2026-10-05). It routes `.kicad_pcb` boards and judges the result with KiCad's own design-rule check; on its PCBench run it reports 54.5% clean boards on the hardest tier against 36.4% for Freerouting 2.5, and it states its limits (85% clean placement on held-out designs against a 90% target, 41% on dense packages, more vias). The author's [post](https://x.com/VectorCrossProd/status/2108168028282315081) of 2026-10-08 announces release 0.9.0, which builds on macOS with a Metal GPU backend. The repository has a `CLAUDE.md` with guidance for implementation sessions, and on 2026-10-08, 10 of its 252 commits named Claude Opus 5.5 as co-author. It is a routing engine, not an LLM tool; agents can call its CLI.

### 2D image, video and design tooling

- [storytold/photocraft](https://github.com/storytold/photocraft): "An open-source, clean-room reimplementation of Adobe Photoshop in pure Rust", per the repository (MIT or Apache-2.0; about 7,200 stars on 2026-10-06; created 2026-09-30; "early alpha"). It edits layered PSD files, and re-saving "keeps the render of 307 of the 309 psd-tools test files". "Every menu item, tool and dialog runs a command from one registry of 500+ commands"; the UI, the CLI, a JSON control channel and an MCP server (`photocraft-cli mcp`) call the same commands, "so anything you can click, a script or an AI agent can do too". Release 0.2.0 has desktop installers and a WebAssembly build "to host yourself"; there is no hosted demo. Its `AGENTS.md` is a "guide for AI agents and contributors", and on 2026-10-06, 152 of its 279 commits named a Claude model as co-author, mostly Claude Opus 5.5. It is one of the Crafting Apps (next entry); [its page](https://getartcraft.com/apps/photocraft) has the downloads.
- [Crafting Apps](https://getartcraft.com/apps): "Image editing, vector illustration, video, photography, PDFs, motion graphics and page layout: seven native, open-source apps from the ArtCraft team, built in Rust and free to use", per the page. Each repository calls its app a clean-room reimplementation of an Adobe application: PhotoCraft (Photoshop), [VectorCraft](https://github.com/storytold/vectorcraft) (Illustrator), [FilmCraft](https://github.com/storytold/filmcraft) (Premiere Pro), [LightCraft](https://github.com/storytold/lightcraft) (Lightroom), [PrintCraft](https://github.com/storytold/printcraft) (Acrobat), [EffectCraft](https://github.com/storytold/effectcraft) (After Effects) and [DesignCraft](https://github.com/storytold/designcraft) (InDesign). All seven are MIT or Apache-2.0, were created on 2026-09-30 or 2026-10-01, and are marked early alpha or in development. The page says "Drive every app from a CLI, a JSON control channel or an MCP server, built for automation and AI agents", and that all but FilmCraft "also compile to WebAssembly and run in a browser tab"; none is hosted online. On 2026-10-06, 2,262 of the 3,188 commits across the seven repositories named a Claude model as co-author, mostly Claude Opus 5.5.
- [Shaders](https://github.com/shader-effects-inc/shaders): "WebGPU components for React, Vue, Svelte, Solid, JS & Framer", per the repository (MIT; created 2026-09-29). The vendor's [announcement](https://x.com/npm_i_shaders/status/2107467668009963819) of 2026-10-06 says "Shaders is now open source" and offers "200+ WebGPU components"; [a reply](https://x.com/npm_i_shaders/status/2107467680022479108) says agents "can now build on production-tested components" instead of raw WGSL, and [another](https://x.com/npm_i_shaders/status/2107467691728810418) links the [announcement page](https://shaders.com/updates/shaders-is-open-source). For agents, the repository offers a skill (`npx shaders skill`), an MCP server (`npx shaders@latest install-mcp`) with which "your agent can find, install and edit the shaders you design", and llms.txt. The design editor at [shaders.com](https://shaders.com) needs an account, and Shaders Pro presets stay paid.

## Resources

Collections that models and agents can draw on. They are not cases and carry no rank.

- [Circuit World (HeyPCB)](https://heypcb.ai/world): "Boards published by heypcb users, and imported open-source hardware — open one to read its schematic, board and 3D, or fork it to build your own", per the page. The vendor's [announcement](https://x.com/heypcbai/status/2105413626467389450) of 2026-09-30 says it passed "10,000+ open-source boards" across robotics, drones, wearables, keyboards, cameras, IoT and power; the count is the vendor's, and a [post by Justin Kim](https://x.com/justinplaygame/status/2105414286650794002), who builds HeyPCB, shares it. HeyPCB sells an AI PCB designer that outputs KiCad files.
- [Canvas (HeyPCB)](https://heypcb.ai/canvas): "Post what you are building — a board, a schematic, a scope trace, a question — and hardware people answer", per the page. The vendor's [announcement](https://x.com/heypcbai/status/2106130836584304667) of 2026-10-02 describes a place to talk about hardware, discover what others are building and "Fork a project and build on it together in browser EDA + MCAD".
- [Explore (HeyPCB)](https://heypcb.ai/explore): "Circuit World as a map, grouped by what the boards are — and the notes people wrote about them, a question, a thought, a tip, with the replies under each one", per the page. The vendor's [article](https://x.com/heypcbai/status/2107225352288502057) of 2026-10-05, "We built a map of open-source hardware", describes "11,000+ boards. One map." and says a board opened in the HeyPCB editor can be inspected, modified or discussed with its assistant ("ask Inky questions about the hardware"); it names no AI model.
- [Robotich](https://robotich.co): "Explore open-source robots in 3D and audit their parts, motion, and technical evidence", per the page. Daniel Dominguez's [announcement](https://x.com/dominguezdaniel/status/2107839381675335782) of 2026-10-07 calls it "a build-focused technical platform for open-source robotics" to "explore source repos, CAD, parts, mechanisms, and manufacturing context to understand and build real robots". The post does not name a model or say how the robot data were compiled.

## Coverage and Roundups

- [Nipale-ai: GLM-5.3 game](https://nipale-ai.github.io/glm-5-3-one-prompt-game/) and [Claude Fable 5.1 game](https://nipale-ai.github.io/fable-5-1-one-prompt-game/): creator-documented articles, both dated 2026-09-01. Models: GLM-5.3 and Claude Fable 5.1, author-labelled effort "high". The author describes "Same prompt, same effort level, same output cap, same machine." and separately credits "Blender 5.2", "FLUX.2 Q8", "LTX-2.5" and "ACE-Step" for geometry, images, video and audio. These are single-author experiments, not a statistical model ranking; the games have not been independently played. Code and prompts: [GLM repository](https://github.com/Nipale-ai/glm-5-3-one-prompt-game), [Fable repository](https://github.com/Nipale-ai/fable-5-1-one-prompt-game).
- [GIGAZINE, 2026-09-07: examples of GPT-6 Astra's 3D modeling capabilities](https://www.gigazine.net/gsc_news/en/20260907-gpt-6-astra-3d-modeling/)
- [nativefirst.ai: "GPT-6 Astra Turned One Sketch Into 3,295 Editable Objects"](https://nativefirst.ai/blog/astra-blender-3295-editable-objects/)
- [Jayant Solanki: "Built With GPT-6 Astra: What People Are Actually Shipping"](https://thejayant.in/blog/built-with-gpt-6-astra)
- [npaka (note.com, Japanese): GPT-6 Astra starts using robots](https://note.com/npaka/n/n8058267c6f69)
- [RoboLab and GPT-6 Astra as an embodied policy, report commentary](https://x.com/mli0603/status/2099370170649333807): Commentary on the already archived "GPT 6 Astra as an Embodied Policy" report, with its RoboLab graphic; no independent experiment. (Max Zhaoshuo Li 李赵硕 (@mli0603), 2026-09-14)
- [How to Turn GPT-6 Astra Into Your Own 3D Studio](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=4WfAphCZXp0): A how-to workflow covering the Higgsfield plugin, character animation, product work and camera moves. (Jack Vs. AI, 2026-09-13)
- [【神AIツール】ChatGPT最新モデル『GPT-6 Astra』の性能が高過ぎる。Fable5.1超え？ポケモン風3Dゲーム、Blender、LP、提案資料まで。業務で使える事例をデモ付きで爆速紹介](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=3o84TYn4jYc): A Japanese launch review spanning original 3D demos and unrelated applications. (チャエン【AI研究所】〜仕事で使える最新のAI情報を発信〜 Byデジライズ, 2026-09-05)
- [Full GPT Astra + Blender + Seedance 2.5 Tutorial for AI Video Automation](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=9fRm6UNdEz4): A Blender/Seedance instructional workflow with downloadable files, not a separately bounded original case. (The Cutting Edge School, 2026-09-07)
- [New GPT-6 Astra Takes Over 3D + Blender](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=I9Oc5AwAxBw): A sponsored multi-test Blender workflow/review covering setup, characters, scenes, retopology, a locomotive and video finishing. (PixelArtistry, 2026-09-13)
- [Can GPT-6 Astra Animate Characters In Blender?](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=mTEw45oqio4): A setup-and-prompt tutorial for animating an existing rigged character. The same creator's Blender cathedral and character-animation comparison is listed among the 3D cases above. (Aidan Stanik, 2026-09-08)
- [GPT-6 Astra: 20 Real Examples From Useful to Almost Impossible](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=_AyXuJKm8iw): A broad roundup with explicit links to demonstrations already listed above, among them the Palace of Fine Arts reconstruction, the Unreal survival world, the Van Gogh walkable town and the 3D iPod-style Codex interface. (The AI Advantage, 2026-09-04)
- [ASTRA IS HERE (GPT-6 RELEASED)](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=xdXLzFzxA9Q): General launch review with a Sim City chapter that revisits the creator's own SimCity- and Fall-Guys-style prototypes listed above. (Matthew Berman, 2026-09-03)
- [GPT-6 Astra Made This Entire Video](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=dT5-x3u5nCg): A video-production walkthrough that compiles other creators' Astra demonstrations; coverage rather than original 3D, CAD or robot work. (Nate Herk | AI Automation, 2026-09-04)
- [GPT-6 Astra Is Finally Here (And It's REALLY Good)](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=GGzT7zVrRTU): A general first-look/launch review. Its metadata does not establish a specific independent new 3D artifact. (Matt Wolfe, 2026-09-03)
- [We asked for your wildest 3D builds with GPT-6 Astra. You delivered.](https://x.com/OpenAIDevs/status/2101113172619100354): OpenAI Developers' roundup of community builds, 2026-09-19. The individual builds keep their creators' attribution.
- [Game Dev Is DEAD? GPT-6 Astra Built This](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=zmxny_AApo8): AI BROS, 2026-09-20, an arena-shooter prototype combining a browser build with Blender and Meshy integrations; title, channel and upload date read from YouTube, video not watched.
- [GPT-Astra in Architecture: Agentic AI Modeling with Astra, Fable, Rhino and Revit](https://x.com/PAACADEMYY/status/2103563660769874008): PAACADEMY, 2026-09-25, announces a five-hour live workshop on October 4, 2026, led by Fredy Fortich, on driving Rhino and Revit through MCP for a mid-rise housing block. Its topics include "Benchmark GPT-6 Astra and Claude Fable 5 on identical architectural tasks". It is an announcement with no results yet; [course page](https://paacademy.com/course/GPT6-Astra-Architecture-AI-Modeling-Fable-Rhino-Revit).
- [Claude + Revit MCP: Autodesk Made It Read-Only on Purpose. The Internet Didn't Get the Memo.](https://x.com/Solvaix/status/2083614624423796738): Solvaix, 2026-08-01, an X article arguing that the official Revit MCP server, a tech preview released with Revit 2027, is read-only by design, while community-built servers add write access. It summarizes other people's tests, including repeated failures on stairs, and runs no experiment of its own.

## Related research

- [Articraft: An Agentic System for Scalable Articulated 3D Asset Generation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.15187), Matt Zhou, Ruining Li, Xiaoyang Lyu, Zhaomou Song, Zhening Huang, Chuanxia Zheng, Christian Rupprecht, Andrea Vedaldi and Shangzhe Wu; arXiv v1 submitted 2026-05-14. An LLM agent writes programs against an SDK for parts, geometry, joints and tests, and a harness validates the articulated assets and returns structured feedback; the [reference code](https://github.com/mattzh72/articraft) is Apache-2.0, and the maintained [Articraft tool](https://github.com/articraftresearch/Articraft) (Apache-2.0) exports posable USDZ files and defaults to GPT-6 Astra at high effort.
- [HARMONY: Hierarchical Agentic Reasoning for MONocular Image-to-Scene Synthesis](https://cwchenwang.github.io/harmony/), Shufan Sun, Chen Wang, Enxin Song, Jiatao Gu and Lingjie Liu; [arXiv v1](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.26793) submitted 2026-09-22. It reconstructs an editable indoor 3D scene from a single image by placing objects hierarchically with VLM reasoning and refining them against predicted geometry, with [MIT-licensed code](https://github.com/ShufanSun/harmony-release) and the [HARMONY300 benchmark](https://huggingface.co/datasets/ShufanSun/harmony).
- [Agent as Policy for Robotic Manipulation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.12541), Mengzhao Jia, Yang Lin, Xixin Zhang, Zhihan Zhang, Xiaobai Liu and Meng Jiang; arXiv v1 submitted 2026-09-11. The authors use GPT-6 Astra at high effort through Codex as the main agent, with model and effort comparisons, to observe, program, act and verify on real robots. They report assembly, block construction, dice and towel-manipulation experiments; Table 2 reports per-model two-pair assembly results. [Code](https://github.com/agent-as-policy-2026/agent-as-policy) is Apache-2.0; [project](https://agent-as-policy-2026.github.io/) and [dataset](https://huggingface.co/datasets/Agent-as-Policy/agent-as-policy) provide demonstrations and execution artifacts. The physical setup uses I2RT YAM arms, wrist RealSense D405 cameras and a top Logitech BRIO camera; reproducing physical trials requires compatible hardware, camera/robot calibration and model access.
- [Coding Agents for Generalized Task and Motion Planning Problems](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.30233), Matteo Merler, Bowen Li, Josh Roy, Yichao Liang, Qianwei Wang, Yixuan Huang and Tom Silver; arXiv v1 submitted 2026-09-24. Claude Code with Opus 5 and Codex with GPT-5.6 Sol and GPT-6 Astra each write one program per simulated task-and-motion-planning environment, and the authors evaluate "980 generated programs on 100 held-out instances each". States are fully observed and object-centric, so perception is outside the study. The [project page](https://agenticgentamp.github.io/) has an interactive gallery of rollouts and the results table; the [code](https://github.com/tomsilver/robocode) is MIT. Mean success figures are in [Benchmarks](#benchmarks).
- [AHa-3D: Agentic Tool Use for Real2Sim with GPT-6 Astra](https://kevinxu02.github.io/real2sim-indoor-site/), Congrong Xu, Siyuan Bian and Jun Gao (equal contribution); a research blog read on 2026-09-27, with no announcement post found. Its summary: "Converting videos into simulatable environments through harness with agentic tool use and GPT-6 Astra." The harness gives Astra camera estimation, segmentation, fused reference meshes, measurement tools and a furniture and material library, in an observe, build, verify, interact and deliver workflow that turns a room video into an editable Blender scene with physics, lights, doors and human or robot motion. An ablation compares two medium-effort Astra runs on the same video with and without the harness, with token counts per stage. The page hosts an interactive scene, and the [code](https://github.com/KevinXu02/aha-3d) is Apache-2.0.
- [EPICCAD: editable CAD histories and constraints](https://link.springer.com/article/10.1007/s44163-026-01899-5), Zehe Yin, Weixin Lin and Xiangbiao Kong, published 2026-09-02. The authors describe "a multimodal benchmark for history-based CAD modeling". Data and code are "available from the corresponding author on reasonable request." Original proprietary Siemens NX files cannot be redistributed; derived industrial assets have separate sharing conditions. This is research infrastructure, with no openly downloadable release verified, rather than a frontier-model demonstration.
- [Procedura: Agentic 3D Modeling with Procedural Control](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.26238), Youtian Lin and colleagues, arXiv v1 submitted 2026-08-26. The authors describe assemblies whose "named parts are joined by typed, machine-checkable mates." The reported gates are "compile, mate, and connectivity checks". The [project page](https://spatiaos.github.io/projects/procedura/) names "Gemini 3.7 Flash" and "GPT‑5.6‑sol" configurations and reports "simulator‑validated articulation". Results are author-reported; simulator checks do not establish universal manufacturability or physical accuracy. The Code link returns to the project page, and no implementation repository was verified.

## About the Archived Media

Video links open the file from this repository's GitHub Pages site (https://frank-zy-dou.github.io/awesome-ai-3d-modeling-robotics/), which serves them as playable MP4; the same files sit under `assets/videos` in the repository tree. The videos are re-encoded copies of the media attached to the linked posts (H.264, at most 540p; new clips longer than two minutes are stored at 360p). Thumbnails for new clips use the frame at 30 per cent of their duration. Images are resized to at most 1280 px wide. Nothing is cut or edited. Authors who want a file removed can open an issue.

## Contributing

Open a pull request that adds a row to the matching table with the original post URL (not a repost), the author, the date, one or two sentences that stick to what the post says, the tools the post names, and an archived copy of the media under `assets/`. Reposts and follow-ups go on the original case's row. Benchmark numbers need a link to the page where the number appears. Link the code the author published for the demo, or for the workflow or evaluation that produced it, and any live demo website. Within every subsection, posts with such code come first, then posts with a live demo website, then posts alone.
